<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We disprove Tachikawa's second conjecture by constructing a finite-dimensional symmetric algebra over $`k=\mathbb F_2(q,H_1,H_2)`$ with a finite-dimensional nonprojective module whose self-extension groups vanish in every positive degree. The associated endomorphism algebra also gives counterexamples to the classical, generalized, and strong Nakayama conjectures, the Auslander–Gorenstein conjecture, and the Wakamatsu tilting conjecture. These conclusions persist after every extension of <i>k</i>.

- **claim-symmetric-counterexample** (counterexample, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $$k=\mathbb F_2(q,H_1,H_2),$$ where $q,H_1,H_2$ are algebraically independent. There exist a finite-dimensional associative unital symmetric $k$-algebra $A$ and a finite-dimensional unital left $A$-module $M$ such that $M$ is nonprojective and $$\mathop{\mathrm{Ext}}_A^i(M,M)=0\qquad\text{for every integer }i>0.$$ In particular, Tachikawa’s second conjecture is false.
  - Explanation: A finite-dimensional symmetric algebra admits a finite-dimensional nonprojective module whose positive self-extensions all vanish.
- **claim-homological-conjectures** (counterexample, [source](#stmt-cor-homological)): Let $A,M$ be as in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>. For every field extension $K/k$, put $A_K=K\otimes_k A$, $M_K=K\otimes_k M$, and $$\Gamma_K=\mathop{\mathrm{End}}_{A_K}(A_K\oplus M_K)^{\mathrm{op}}.$$ Then $\Gamma_K$ is a finite-dimensional $K$-algebra with the following properties, in the category of finite left $\Gamma_K$-modules.* 1. *Every term of the minimal injective resolution of $\Gamma_K$ is projective, but $\mathop{\mathrm{id}}_{\Gamma_K}\Gamma_K=\infty$.* 2. *There is a nonzero simple module $S$ such that $\mathop{\mathrm{Ext}}_{\Gamma_K}^i(S,\Gamma_K)=0$ for every $i\geq0$.* 3. *There is a projective-injective Wakamatsu tilting module $W$ which is not tilting.* *Consequently the classical, generalized, and strong Nakayama conjectures, the Auslander–Gorenstein conjecture, and the Wakamatsu tilting conjecture are false for finite-dimensional algebras over $K$.
  - Explanation: The opposite endomorphism algebra supplies failures of the Nakayama, Auslander–Gorenstein, and Wakamatsu tilting conjectures over every extension of the stated field.
- **claim-infinite-finitistic-dimensions** (construction, [source](#stmt-cor-finitistic)): Let $k$ be the field in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, let $K/k$ be any field extension, and let $\Gamma_K$ be the algebra in Corollary <a href="#cor:homological" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:homological">2</a>. For every integer $n\geq1$, there is a finite-dimensional left $\Gamma_K$-module $C_n$ such that $$\mathop{\mathrm{pd}}_{\Gamma_K}C_n=n.$$ Consequently $\Gamma_K$ has infinite little left finitistic dimension and infinite big left finitistic dimension.
  - Explanation: Finite modules with every positive finite projective dimension force both left finitistic dimensions of the associated algebra to be infinite.
- **claim-complete-resolution-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-h-comparison)): Let $P$ be totally acyclic and let $Q$ be an exact complex of projectives. For every integer $a$ there are natural isomorphisms $$\label{h:comparison-formula} H^a\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_G^\bullet(P,Q) \simeq\underline{\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}}_G(c_aP,c_0Q) \simeq H^a\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_G(P,c_0Q).$$ The first isomorphism is induced by cokernels of shifted chain maps and respects composition. For $a>0$ these spaces are $\mathop{\mathrm{Ext}}_G^a(c_0P,c_0Q)$.
  - Explanation: Cokernels identify full Hom cohomology with stable maps in every degree, compatibly with composition; positive degrees also compute ordinary extensions.
- **claim-symmetric-stable-duality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-h-duality)): For a symmetric algebra $G$ there are natural isomorphisms $$\label{h:duality-formula} D\underline{\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}}_G(M,N)\simeq\underline{\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}}_G(N,M[-1]).$$ There is a linear functional $\lambda_M:\underline{\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}}_G(M,M[-1])\to k$ for which the perfect pairing is $(f,g)\mapsto\lambda_M(gf)$. Consequently, for every integer $a$ the pairing $$\label{h:shifted-pairing} \underline{\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}}_G(M,N[a])\times\underline{\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}}_G(N,M[-a-1])\longrightarrow k, \qquad (\alpha,\beta)\longmapsto\lambda_M\bigl(\beta[a]\alpha\bigr)$$ is perfect and natural.
  - Explanation: Symmetry supplies a natural perfect composition pairing between stable maps in complementary shifted degrees.
- **claim-derived-projective-models** (construction, [source](#stmt-h-derived-models)): A bounded-below complex $P$ of projectives computes derived maps out of it by chain maps modulo homotopy. Every bounded complex of finite modules has such a projective model with finite terms. In particular, two such models of the same derived object are homotopy equivalent.* *Suppose a derived triangle is given by $$L\longrightarrow V\longrightarrow R\xrightarrow{\partial}L[1].$$ For projective models $P_L,P_R$ and a chain map $b:P_R\to P_L[1]$ representing $\partial$, a model of $V$ is the two-cell complex $$\label{h:two-cell} P_{V,j}=P_{L,j}\oplus P_{R,j},\qquad d_j^{P_V}=\begin{pmatrix}d_j^{P_L}&b_j\\0&d_j^{P_R}\end{pmatrix}.$$ Its subcomplex and quotient represent $L$ and $R$, and its connecting map is the specified $\partial$.
  - Explanation: Bounded-below projective models compute derived maps, and a representative of the connecting morphism determines a model of the entire triangle.
- **claim-functorial-stable-images** (construction, [source](#stmt-h-stable-image)): Let $G$ be symmetric and let $P$ be bounded below with projective terms and bounded homology. For $n$ sufficiently large the formula $$\label{h:stable-image-formula} \langle P\rangle=(c_nP)[n]$$ defines a stable module, independent of $n$ up to natural stable isomorphism. It is functorial on derived maps represented by such models, commutes with shifts, sends a module to its stable class, and sends an Ext class to its corresponding stable map.* *A degreewise split short exact sequence $0\to P_L\to P_V\to P_R\to0$ of these complexes gives a stable connecting map $\langle P_R\rangle\to\langle P_L\rangle[1]$ and the associated long exact stable Hom sequences. With respect to a graded splitting, this connecting map is the stable image of the cross differential $b:P_R\to P_L[1]$ in <a href="#h:two-cell" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="h:two-cell">[h:two-cell]</a>.
  - Explanation: High cokernels give stable images independent of the truncation choice, retaining shifts, extension classes, and the actual connecting maps.
- **claim-perfect-factorization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-h-perfect-factor)): Over a symmetric algebra, a derived map between bounded derived objects whose stable image is zero factors in the derived category through a perfect complex.
  - Explanation: A vanishing stable image forces a derived map between bounded objects to factor through a perfect complex.
- **claim-finite-shifts-evaluation** (construction, [source](#stmt-h-evaluation)): Every stable shift of a finite side-projective bimodule has a finite side-projective representative. Evaluation at a finite $E$-module $X$ sends stable maps between these bimodules to stable $E$-module maps and has natural identifications $$\label{h:evaluation-shifts} (L[a])X\simeq(LX)[a],\qquad L(X[a])\simeq(LX)[a].$$ It preserves the stable connecting maps of short exact sequences of side-projective bimodules, and these identifications are natural in both $L$ and $X$.* *If $P$ is a bounded-below complex of projective bimodules with bounded homology and all sufficiently high $c_nP$ are side-projective, then $P\otimes_EX$ has bounded homology and $$\label{h:evaluation-image} \langle P\rangle X\simeq\langle P\otimes_EX\rangle,$$ naturally on maps and on connecting maps from degreewise split sequences of such complexes. The hypothesis on the high cokernels holds whenever $P$, considered separately on either side, is homotopy equivalent to a bounded complex of projectives on that side.
  - Explanation: Finite side-projective representatives support stable shifts and evaluation, including compatibility with connecting morphisms and high-cokernel stable images.
- **claim-trivial-extension-symmetry** (construction, [source](#stmt-tr-trivial-symmetric)): The trivial extension $R\ltimes DR$ is a finite-dimensional symmetric algebra. In particular, its left and right regular modules are injective in the categories of all unital modules.
  - Explanation: The dual bimodule trivial extension is symmetric, and its regular modules are injective even when arbitrary unital modules are allowed.
- **claim-two-sided-transfer** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-tr-transfer)): Let $P$ be a totally acyclic complex of finite projective left $R$-modules, and put $Z=c_0P$. Suppose that $Z$ is nonprojective and $$\label{tr:two-sided-vanishing} H^a\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_R(P,Z)=0 \qquad\text{for }a>0\text{ and for }a\leq -2.$$ Then $$A=R\ltimes DR,\qquad M=A\otimes_R Z$$ are a finite-dimensional symmetric algebra and a finite-dimensional nonprojective left $A$-module satisfying $$\mathop{\mathrm{Ext}}_A^a(M,M)=0\qquad(a>0).$$
  - Explanation: Positive and sufficiently negative complete self-Hom vanishing transfer nonprojectivity and positive self-extension vanishing to the symmetric trivial extension.
- **claim-starting-algebra-table** (construction, [source](#stmt-a-table)): The table <a href="#a:multiplication" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="a:multiplication">[a:multiplication]</a> defines an associative unital algebra of dimension ten. Its radical is the span of the eight basis vectors other than $e,f$, and $(\mathop{\mathrm{rad}}C)^5=0$.
  - Explanation: The displayed multiplication defines the starting associative unital algebra, with the stated radical and nilpotence relation.
- **claim-dual-projective-dimensions** (bound, [source](#stmt-a-dual-dimensions)): The dual regular module $DC$ has projective dimension at most two on both sides. More precisely, $$D(eC)\cong Ce,\qquad D(Ce)\cong eC,$$ and there are exact sequences $$\begin{aligned} 0\longrightarrow Cf\xrightarrow{\rho_t}Ce \xrightarrow{\rho_{\ell_{-2}}}Ce \xrightarrow{e\mapsto j^*}D(fC)\longrightarrow0, \label{a:left-dual-resolution}\\ 0\longrightarrow fC\xrightarrow{\lambda_u}eC \xrightarrow{\lambda_{\ell_0}}eC \xrightarrow{e\mapsto v^*}D(Cf)\longrightarrow0. \label{a:right-dual-resolution} \end{aligned}$$ Consequently $$\mathop{\mathrm{pd}}_B({}_B E)\le4,\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{pd}}_{B^{\mathrm{op}}}(E_B)\le4,\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{id}}_B({}_B B)\le4. \label{a:dimension-bounds}$$
  - Explanation: Explicit resolutions of the dual regular modules control the projective dimensions needed for induction and the injective dimension needed for the trace reduction.
- **claim-simple-resolution** (construction, [source](#stmt-a-resolution)): The augmentation $Cf\to s$ extends to a projective resolution $R\to s$ with $$R_0=Cf,\qquad R_i=Ce\ (i\ge1),\qquad d_1=\rho_u,\qquad d_{i+2}=\rho_{\ell_i}\ (i\ge0). \label{a:simple-resolution}$$ It satisfies $$H^a\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_C(R,s)= \begin{cases}k&a=0,\\0&a\ne0,\end{cases} \qquad H^a\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_C(R,C)= \begin{cases}Ds&a=2,\\0&a\ne2,\end{cases} \label{a:dual-cohomology}$$ where the second identification is an isomorphism of right $C$-modules.
  - Explanation: The parameter-shifting multiplication maps resolve the simple module and determine its dual cohomology as a right module.
- **claim-polynomial-yoneda-algebra** (construction, [source](#stmt-e-polynomial)): Put $\mathcal J=T\otimes_C R$, where $R$ is the resolution in <a href="#a:simple-resolution" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="a:simple-resolution">[a:simple-resolution]</a>. There is a triangle in the derived category of $T$-modules $$s[2]\longrightarrow\mathcal J\longrightarrow s \xrightarrow{\tau}s[3], \label{e:triangle}$$ and the connecting class generates the Yoneda algebra: $$\mathop{\mathrm{Ext}}_T^*(s,s)=k[\tau],\qquad \|\tau\|=3. \label{e:one-polynomial}$$ The complex $\mathcal J$ is bounded below, has finite projective $T$-terms, and has bounded homology.
  - Explanation: Derived induction produces a triangle whose connecting class generates the polynomial self-extension algebra of the simple module.
- **claim-stable-self-map-profile** (classification, [source](#stmt-e-all-degrees)): The module $X$ is nonprojective and $$\mathcal H^{\ge0}=k[\tau_1,\tau_2],\qquad \|\tau_1\|=\|\tau_2\|=3. \label{e:two-polynomial}$$ In every integer degree, the only nonzero groups are $$\mathcal H^{3m}=\mathcal P_m,\qquad \mathcal H^{-3m-1}=D\mathcal P_m\qquad(m\ge0). \label{e:profile}$$ Under these identifications, postcomposition by a shifted $\tau_i$ between negative groups is the transpose of multiplication by $\tau_i$ on the polynomial spaces. The maps $\mathcal H^{-1}\to\mathcal H^2$ given by either class are zero.
  - Explanation: The nonnegative stable self-maps form a polynomial algebra, while symmetric duality determines the negative groups and their multiplication maps.
- **claim-socle-stable-generator** (construction, [source](#stmt-e-socle)): Put $$p_f=f\otimes f,\qquad \zeta=f^*\otimes f^*\in E p_f.$$ For every $a,b\in E$, $a\zeta b=\chi(a)\chi(b)\zeta$. The map $i_X:X\to Ep_f$, $i_X(1)=\zeta$, is an embedding with image in $(\mathop{\mathrm{rad}}E)p_f=\Omega X$. Regarded as a map $X\to\Omega X$, it is nonzero stably and spans $\mathcal H^{-1}$.
  - Explanation: The explicit socle embedding into the syzygy is nonzero modulo projective factorizations and generates the indicated negative stable group.
- **claim-twist-scalar-actions** (classification, [source](#stmt-e-twists)): For every $H\in k^\times$ and every $m\ge0$, the action of $U_H(-)$ on the groups in <a href="#e:profile" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="e:profile">[e:profile]</a> is $$\left.U_H\right\|_{\mathcal H^{3m}}=H^{-m}\,1_{\mathcal P_m}, \qquad \left.U_H\right\|_{\mathcal H^{-3m-1}}=H^{m+2}\,1_{D\mathcal P_m}. \label{e:twist-weights}$$ In particular, the negative action is the stated scalar on the entire $(m+1)$-dimensional space.
  - Explanation: The twists act by scalar powers on entire positive polynomial spaces and their negative dual spaces.
- **claim-side-projective-bimodule-lifts** (construction, [source](#stmt-b-bimodule)): There is a finite $E$-bimodule $Y$, projective separately as a left and as a right $E$-module, such that $$\label{b:W} W^a:=\underline{\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}}_E(X,(YX)[a]) \cong \begin{cases} k,&a=-3,0,\\ 0,&a\notin\{-3,0\}. \end{cases}$$ For every $H\in k^\times$, there is an ordinary bimodule map $g_H:U_H\to Y$ whose evaluation, under $U_HX=X$, represents a nonzero element of $W^0$. The same $Y$ works for all $H$, and all modules and maps are defined over $k$.
  - Explanation: A fixed finite side-projective bimodule has the sparse evaluated Hom profile and receives genuine bimodule maps from every twist with nonzero evaluations.
- **claim-finite-bar-representative** (construction, [source](#stmt-b-bar-model)): The complex $K$ represents $E\otimes_B^{\mathbf L}E$, has finite projective $E^e$-terms and has homology only in degrees $0,\ldots,4$. The bimodule $c_5K$ is projective on both sides. Consequently its stable image $$\mathcal I=\langle K\rangle=(c_5K)[5]$$ has finite side-projective representatives, as does $\mathcal I[-1]$. One such representative $Y$ of $\mathcal I[-1]$ is obtained by taking four successive finite bimodule cosyzygies of $c_5K$.
  - Explanation: Bounded homology and separate side projectivity of a specified bar cokernel provide a finite representative of shifted derived induction.
- **claim-evaluated-bimodule-profile** (classification, [source](#stmt-b-profile)): The bimodule $Y$ of Lemma <a href="#b:bar-model" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="b:bar-model">20</a> satisfies <a href="#b:W" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="b:W">[b:W]</a>.
  - Explanation: The evaluated finite bimodule has precisely the stable Hom profile required for the subsequent fiber construction.
- **claim-ordinary-perfect-factorization** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-b-ordinary-factorization)): If a bimodule map $v:B\to DB$ factors in the derived category through a perfect $B^e$-complex, then it factors by ordinary bimodule maps through an object of $\mathop{\mathrm{add}}(DB\otimes B)$, where the tensor product has its outer bimodule actions.
  - Explanation: A perfect derived factorization of the indicated bimodule map can be replaced by an ordinary factorization through the specified additive class.
- **claim-character-trace-obstruction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-b-trace-obstruction)): The bimodule map $$\label{b:character-map} \eta:B\longrightarrow DB,\qquad \eta(a)=\chi_B(a)\chi_B,$$ does not factor in the derived category through a perfect $B^e$-complex.
  - Explanation: The character map cannot factor through a perfect bimodule complex; ordinary trace vanishing would contradict its character value.
- **claim-stable-tensor-hom-adjunction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-b-stable-adjunction)): For every finite side-projective bimodule $L$, ordinary tensor–Hom adjunction induces a natural isomorphism $$\underline{\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}}_{E^e}(L,S)\ \cong\ \underline{\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}}_E(LX,X).$$ This assertion does not require $S$ to be side-projective.
  - Explanation: For a side-projective source, tensor–Hom adjunction identifies the stable factorization ideals without requiring side projectivity of the target.
- **claim-nonzero-evaluated-lift** (construction, [source](#stmt-b-evaluated-lift)): For every $H\in k^\times$, there is a stable bimodule map $U_H[1]\to\mathcal I$ with nonzero evaluated stable map $X[1]\to\mathcal IX$.
  - Explanation: The trace obstruction and symmetric duality yield a stable bimodule lift whose evaluation remains nonzero.
- **claim-comparison-map-ranges** (classification, [source](#stmt-t-comparison-maps)): The map $\Delta^0$ is surjective. For every $a>0$ and every $a\leq-3$, $\Delta^a$ is an isomorphism, and $\Delta^{-2}$ is injective.
  - Explanation: Distinct twist scalars give invertibility in the supported nonboundary ranges. The boundary surjectivity follows from the normalized scalar sum, and boundary injectivity follows from a zero source.
- **claim-finite-nonprojective-cone-module** (construction, [source](#stmt-t-finite-module)): The complex $P_Z$ is totally acyclic. Its degree-zero cokernel $Z$ is finite-dimensional and nonprojective.
  - Explanation: The triangular cone is totally acyclic and has a finite nonprojective cokernel, detected by its upper component.
- **claim-complete-self-hom-vanishing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-t-vanishing)): The module and resolution in <a href="#t:cone" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="t:cone">[t:cone]</a> satisfy $$H^a\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_\Lambda(P_Z,Z)=0 \qquad(a>0\ \text{or}\ a\leq-2).$$
  - Explanation: The cone endomorphism sequence and comparison-map boundary checks establish exactly the complete self-Hom vanishing needed for transfer.
- **claim-endomorphism-dimensions** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-endomorphism)): Let $B$ be a finite-dimensional symmetric algebra over a field and let $N$ be a finite-dimensional nonprojective left $B$-module satisfying $\mathop{\mathrm{Ext}}_B^i(N,N)=0$ for all $i>0$. Put $$G=B\oplus N,\qquad \Gamma=\mathop{\mathrm{End}}_B(G)^{\mathrm{op}},$$ where endomorphisms compose in the ordinary order. Then $$\mathop{\mathrm{domdim}}({}_\Gamma\Gamma)=\infty, \qquad \mathop{\mathrm{id}}_\Gamma\Gamma=\infty.$$ In particular, $\Gamma$ is not self-injective.
  - Explanation: The opposite endomorphism algebra of a symmetric self-orthogonal generator has infinite dominant dimension while remaining non-self-injective with infinite left self-injective dimension.
- **claim-infinitely-many-tilting-complements** (construction, [source](#stmt-cor-tilting-complements)): For every field extension $K/k$, let $\Gamma_K$ be the algebra in Corollary <a href="#cor:homological" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:homological">2</a>. There is a finite-dimensional projective almost complete tilting left $\Gamma_K^{\mathrm{op}}$-module $P_K$ with infinitely many pairwise nonisomorphic indecomposable complements.
  - Explanation: Generalized Nakayama failure and finite-dimensional duality produce a projective almost complete tilting module with infinitely many distinct indecomposable complements.
- **claim-arbitrary-idempotent-stratifications** (construction, [source](#stmt-cor-stratifying)): For every field extension $K/k$ and every integer $L\geq1$, there is an auxiliary finite-dimensional indecomposable symmetric $K$-algebra $T_L$ admitting an idempotent stratification of length at least $L$ in the sense of Chen, Fang and Xi. In particular, $T_L$ has a nonzero proper stratifying ideal, and $\mathcal D(T_L\text{-}\mathrm{Mod})$ admits a chain of at least $L$ nontrivial standard recollements through successive corner algebras. Here $\mathrm{Mod}$ denotes all left modules and $\mathcal D$ is the unbounded derived category.
  - Explanation: Auxiliary indecomposable symmetric algebras admit arbitrarily long idempotent stratifications and the corresponding successive recollements of unbounded derived categories.

```json
        "exact_quote": "A bounded-below complex $P$ of projectives computes derived maps out of it by chain maps modulo homotopy.           "exact_quote": "A bounded-below complex $P$ of projectives computes derived maps out of it by chain maps modulo homotopy. Every bounded complex of finite modules has such a projective model with finite terms. In particular, two such models of the same derived object are homotopy equivalent.*\n\n\n*Suppose a derived triangle is given by $$L\\longrightarrow V\\longrightarrow R\\xrightarrow{\\partial}L[1].$$ For projective models $P_L,P_R$ and a chain map $b:P_R\\to P_L[1]$ representing $\\partial$, a model of $V$ is the two-cell complex $$\\label{h:two-cell}\n P_{V,j}=P_{L,j}\\oplus P_{R,j},\\qquad\n d_j^{P_V}=\\begin{pmatrix}d_j^{P_L}&b_j\\\\0&d_j^{P_R}\\end{pmatrix}.$$ Its subcomplex and quotient represent $L$ and $R$, and its connecting map is the specified $\\partial$.",
          "source_id": "PM-199.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "A bounded-below complex $P$ of projectives computes derived maps out of it by chain maps modulo homotopy.       "explanation": "Bounded-below projective models compute derived maps, and a representative of the connecting morphism determines a model of the entire triangle.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-h-stable-image",
        "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be symmetric and let $P$ be bounded below with projective terms and bounded homology. For $n$ sufficiently large the formula $$\\label{h:stable-image-formula}\n \\langle P\\rangle=(c_nP)[n]$$ defines a stable module, independent of $n$ up to natural stable isomorphism. It is functorial on derived maps represented by such models, commutes with shifts, sends a module to its stable class, and sends an Ext class to its corresponding stable map.*\n\n*A degreewise split short exact sequence $0\\to P_L\\to P_V\\to P_R\\to0$ of these complexes gives a stable connecting map $\\langle P_R\\rangle\\to\\langle P_L\\rangle[1]$ and the associated long exact stable Hom sequences. With respect to a graded splitting, this connecting map is the stable image of the cross differential $b:P_R\\to P_L[1]$ in <a href=\"#h:two-cell\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"h:two-cell\">[h:two-cell]</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-199.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-h-stable-image",
          "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be symmetric and let $P$ be bounded below with projective terms and bounded homology. For $n$ sufficiently large the formula $$\\label{h:stable-image-formula}\n \\langle P\\rangle=(c_nP)[n]$$ defines a stable module, independent of $n$ up to natural stable isomorphism. It is functorial on derived maps represented by such models, commutes with shifts, sends a module to its stable class, and sends an Ext class to its corresponding stable map.*\n\n*A degreewise split short exact sequence $0\\to P_L\\to P_V\\to P_R\\to0$ of these complexes gives a stable connecting map $\\langle P_R\\rangle\\to\\langle P_L\\rangle[1]$ and the associated long exact stable Hom sequences. With respect to a graded splitting, this connecting map is the stable image of the cross differential $b:P_R\\to P_L[1]$ in <a href=\"#h:two-cell\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"h:two-cell\">[h:two-cell]</a>.",
          "source_id": "PM-199.2"
        }
      ],

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-h-evaluation",
        "exact_quote": "Every stable shift of a finite side-projective bimodule has a finite side-projective representative. Evaluation at a finite $E$-module $X$ sends stable maps between these bimodules to stable $E$-module maps and has natural identifications $$\\label{h:evaluation-shifts}\n (L[a])X\\simeq(LX)[a],\\qquad L(X[a])\\simeq(LX)[a].$$ It preserves the stable connecting maps of short exact sequences of side-projective bimodules, and these identifications are natural in both $L$ and $X$.*\n\n\n*If $P$ is a bounded-below complex of projective bimodules with bounded homology and all sufficiently high $c_nP$ are side-projective, then $P\\otimes_EX$ has bounded homology and $$\\label{h:evaluation-image}\n \\langle P\\rangle X\\simeq\\langle P\\otimes_EX\\rangle,$$ naturally on maps and on connecting maps from degreewise split sequences of such complexes. The hypothesis on the high cokernels holds whenever $P$, considered separately on either side, is homotopy equivalent to a bounded complex of projectives on that side.",
        "source_id": "PM-199.2"
      },

        "exact_quote": "Let $P$ be a totally acyclic complex of finite projective left $R$-modules, and put $Z=c_0P$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-a-dual-dimensions",
        "exact_quote": "The dual regular module $DC$ has projective dimension at most two on both sides. More precisely, $$D(eC)\\cong Ce,\\qquad D(Ce)\\cong eC,$$ and there are exact sequences $$\\begin{aligned}\n0\\longrightarrow Cf\\xrightarrow{\\rho_t}Ce\n \\xrightarrow{\\rho_{\\ell_{-2}}}Ce\n \\xrightarrow{e\\mapsto j^*}D(fC)\\longrightarrow0,\n\\label{a:left-dual-resolution}\\\\\n0\\longrightarrow fC\\xrightarrow{\\lambda_u}eC\n \\xrightarrow{\\lambda_{\\ell_0}}eC\n \\xrightarrow{e\\mapsto v^*}D(Cf)\\longrightarrow0.\n\\label{a:right-dual-resolution}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Consequently $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{pd}}_B({}_B E)\\le4,\\qquad\n\\mathop{\\mathrm{pd}}_{B^{\\mathrm{op}}}(E_B)\\le4,\\qquad\n\\mathop{\\mathrm{id}}_B({}_B B)\\le4.\n\\label{a:dimension-bounds}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-199.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-a-dual-dimensions",
          "exact_quote": "The dual regular module $DC$ has projective dimension at most two on both sides.           "source_id": "PM-199.2"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-a-resolution",
        "exact_quote": "The augmentation $Cf\\to s$ extends to a projective resolution $R\\to s$ with $$R_0=Cf,\\qquad R_i=Ce\\ (i\\ge1),\\qquad\nd_1=\\rho_u,\\qquad d_{i+2}=\\rho_{\\ell_i}\\ (i\\ge0).\n\\label{a:simple-resolution}$$ It satisfies $$H^a\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}_C(R,s)=\n\\begin{cases}k&a=0,\\\\0&a\\ne0,\\end{cases}\n\\qquad\nH^a\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}_C(R,C)=\n\\begin{cases}Ds&a=2,\\\\0&a\\ne2,\\end{cases}\n\\label{a:dual-cohomology}$$ where the second identification is an isomorphism of right $C$-modules.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-e-polynomial",
        "exact_quote": "Put $\\mathcal J=T\\otimes_C R$, where $R$ is the resolution in <a href=\"#a:simple-resolution\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"a:simple-resolution\">[a:simple-resolution]</a>.         "anchor": "stmt-e-all-degrees",
        "exact_quote": "The module $X$ is nonprojective and $$\\mathcal H^{\\ge0}=k[\\tau_1,\\tau_2],\\qquad |\\tau_1|=|\\tau_2|=3.\n\\label{e:two-polynomial}$$ In every integer degree, the only nonzero groups are $$\\mathcal H^{3m}=\\mathcal P_m,\\qquad\n\\mathcal H^{-3m-1}=D\\mathcal P_m\\qquad(m\\ge0).\n\\label{e:profile}$$ Under these identifications, postcomposition by a shifted $\\tau_i$ between negative groups is the transpose of multiplication by $\\tau_i$ on the polynomial spaces. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
```
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Self-orthogonal module** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A finite-dimensional $A$-module $M$ is *self-orthogonal* if $\mathop{\mathrm{Ext}}_A^i(M,M)=0$ for every integer $i>0$.
- **Vector-space duality** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Write $D=\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_k(-,k)$.
- **Symmetric algebra** ([source](#sec:introduction)): An algebra $A$ is *symmetric* if $A\simeq DA$ as $A$-bimodules; in particular, a symmetric algebra is self-injective.
- **Full Hom complex** ([source](#h:full-hom)): For two complexes we use the full Hom complex $$\label{h:full-hom} \mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_G^a(P,Q)=\prod_{j\in\mathbb Z}\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_G(P_j,Q_{j-a}),\qquad (\delta f)_j=d^Q_{j-a}f_j+f_{j-1}d^P_j.$$
- **Stable Hom** ([source](#h:full-hom)): The space $\underline{\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}}_G(M,N)$ is the quotient of $\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_G(M,N)$ by maps factoring through finite projectives.
- **Totally acyclic complex** ([source](#pm-h-9)): An exact complex $P$ of projectives is *totally acyclic* if $\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_G(P,G)$ is exact.
- **Complete resolution** ([source](#pm-h-9)): If $c_0P=M$, we call $P$ a complete resolution of $M$.
- **Stable shifts** ([source](#h:syzygy-sequence)): For such a resolution put $M[a]=c_{-a}P$ in the stable category.
- **Perfect complex** ([source](#pm-h-10)): A complex is *perfect* if it is derived isomorphic to a bounded complex of finite projectives.
- **Stable image of a projective model** ([source](#stmt-h-stable-image)): For $n$ sufficiently large the formula $$\label{h:stable-image-formula} \langle P\rangle=(c_nP)[n]$$ defines a stable module, independent of $n$ up to natural stable isomorphism.
- **Side-projective bimodule** ([source](#pm-h-11)): We call an $E$-bimodule *side-projective* if it is projective as a left and as a right $E$-module, and write $LX=L\otimes_EX$.
- **Trivial extension** ([source](#a:trivial-extension)): Form the trivial extension $$T=C\ltimes DC,\qquad (a,\phi)(b,\psi)=(ab,a\psi+\phi b),\qquad (a\phi b)(c)=\phi(bca). \label{a:trivial-extension}$$
- **Polynomial stable self-map spaces** ([source](#e:stable-groups)): We write $\mathcal P_m$ for the space of polynomials of total exponent $m$ in $\tau_1,\tau_2$; their cohomological degree is $3m$.
- **Twisted regular bimodule** ([source](#e:section)): For $H\in k^\times$, define $$h_H:T\longrightarrow T,\quad (a,\phi)\longmapsto(a,H\phi), \qquad \sigma_H=h_H\otimes h_H, \qquad U_H=E_{\sigma_H}. \label{e:twist-definition}$$
- **Normalized fiber bimodule** ([source](#t:fiber)): Choose a finite free $E$-bimodule $Q$ with a surjection $\pi:Q\to Y$, and define $F$ by $$\label{t:fiber} 0\longrightarrow F\longrightarrow U_1\oplus U_2\oplus Q \xrightarrow{(g_1,g_2,\pi)}Y\longrightarrow0.$$
- **Comparison map** ([source](#t:delta)): Define $$\label{t:delta} \Delta^a:(\mathcal H^a)^2\longrightarrow\mathcal V^a, \qquad (g,h)\longmapsto F(g)v_0+v_0[a]h.$$
- **Triangular algebra** ([source](#t:triangular-algebra)): Form the finite-dimensional algebra $$\label{t:triangular-algebra} \Lambda=\begin{pmatrix}E&0\\F&E\end{pmatrix}.$$
- **Cone module** ([source](#t:cone)): Set $$\label{t:cone} P_Z=\mathop{\mathrm{Cone}}(V),\qquad Z=c_0P_Z.$$
- **Almost complete tilting module** ([source](#sec:consequences)): A finite left module $P$ over a finite-dimensional algebra $\Lambda$ is *almost complete tilting* if it is a direct summand of a tilting module and has one fewer isomorphism class of indecomposable summands than there are isomorphism classes of simple left $\Lambda$-modules.
- **Indecomposable complement** ([source](#sec:consequences)): An *indecomposable complement* is a finite indecomposable module $Y$ such that $P\oplus Y$ is tilting.
- **Stratifying ideal** ([source](#sec:consequences)): For an idempotent $e$ in a finite-dimensional algebra $R$, the ideal $ReR$ is *stratifying* if multiplication induces an isomorphism $$Re\otimes_{eRe}eR\xrightarrow{\sim}ReR$$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{Tor}}_i^{eRe}(Re,eR)=0$ for every $i>0$.

```json
      "interpretation": "The regular left module receives a right action twisted by the simultaneous dual-scaling automorphism.",

        {
          "anchor": "sec:consequences",
          "exact_quote": "A finite left module $P$ over a finite-dimensional algebra $\\Lambda$ is *almost complete tilting* if it is a direct summand of a tilting module and has one fewer isomorphism class of indecomposable summands than there are isomorphism classes of simple left $\\Lambda$-modules.",
          "source_id": "PM-199.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "A finite left module $P$ over a finite-dimensional algebra $\\Lambda$ is *almost complete tilting* if it is a direct summand of a tilting module and has one fewer isomorphism class of indecomposable summands than there are isomorphism classes of simple left $\\Lambda$-modules.",
      "interpretation": "The module is already a tilting summand and lacks an indecomposable summand relative to the simple-module count.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "Almost complete tilting module",
      "scope": "paper-wide"
    },

          "source_id": "PM-199.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "An *indecomposable complement* is a finite indecomposable module $Y$ such that $P\\oplus Y$ is tilting.",
      "interpretation": "Adjoining the complement completes the almost complete module to a tilting module.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "Indecomposable complement",

          "source_id": "PM-199.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "For an idempotent $e$ in a finite-dimensional algebra $R$, the ideal $ReR$ is *stratifying* if multiplication induces an isomorphism $$Re\\otimes_{eRe}eR\\xrightarrow{\\sim}ReR$$ and $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tor}}_i^{eRe}(Re,eR)=0$ for every $i>0$.",
      "interpretation": "Multiplication must identify the tensor product with the ideal, and all positive Tor groups must vanish.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "Stratifying ideal",
```

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-complete-comparison** [claim](#stmt-h-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-h-comparison): Use total acyclicity to extend maps into projectives, lift cokernel maps in both directions, and identify nullhomotopies with projective factorizations. -> Composition-compatible comparison between full Hom cohomology and stable cokernel maps.
- **step-stable-duality** [claim](#stmt-h-duality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-h-duality): Dualize finite projective Hom terms using symmetry and identify the resulting quotient with stable maps into the syzygy. -> Natural perfect composition pairings for shifted stable maps.
- **step-derived-models** [claim](#stmt-h-derived-models) / [proof](#proof-stmt-h-derived-models): Construct bounded-below finite projective models and use the representative connecting map as the cross differential of a cone. -> Projective models computing derived maps and preserving the specified triangle.
- **step-stable-images** [claim](#stmt-h-stable-image) / [proof](#proof-stmt-h-stable-image): Pass to high cokernels above homology support and track the cross differential through the induced short exact sequences. -> Functorial stable images with the actual connecting maps.
- **step-perfect-factorization** [claim](#stmt-h-perfect-factor) / [proof](#proof-stmt-h-perfect-factor): Remove high components by a homotopy and factor the remaining chain map through a bounded projective truncation. -> A perfect derived factorization.
- **step-finite-evaluation** [claim](#stmt-h-evaluation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-h-evaluation): Use finite projective-injective embeddings and separate side splittings to construct shifts and preserve exactness under evaluation. -> Finite representatives and evaluation compatible with shifts and attaching maps.
- **step-trivial-symmetry** [claim](#stmt-tr-trivial-symmetric) / [proof](#proof-stmt-tr-trivial-symmetric): Pair the regular component with its dual and check symmetry and nondegeneracy of the resulting associative form. -> A symmetric trivial extension with injective regular modules.
- **step-transfer** [claim](#stmt-tr-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-tr-transfer): Dualize the complete resolution into an exact injective complex, identify the negative tail with positive extensions into the induced dual summand, and apply induction–restriction adjunction. -> A finite nonprojective self-orthogonal module over a symmetric trivial extension.
- **step-starting-algebra** [claim](#stmt-a-table) / [proof](#proof-stmt-a-table): Use corner compatibility and the homogeneous grading to reduce associativity to the displayed composable triples; identify the nilpotent positive-degree ideal. -> The associative unital starting algebra and its radical.
- **step-dual-resolutions** [claim](#stmt-a-dual-dimensions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-a-dual-dimensions): Compute images and kernels of multiplication maps on both sides, then tensor the finite resolutions and dualize. -> Separate projective-dimension bounds for induced modules and an injective-dimension bound for the starting tensor algebra.
- **step-simple-resolution** [claim](#stmt-a-resolution) / [proof](#proof-stmt-a-resolution): Compute successive images and kernels of parameter-shifting right multiplications and analyze the left-multiplication dual complex. -> An explicit resolution and its dual cohomology with the correct right-module structure.
- **step-polynomial-extensions** [claim](#stmt-e-polynomial) / [proof](#proof-stmt-e-polynomial): Induce the resolution through the coefficient short exact sequence, identify its dual homology, and iterate the connecting-map isomorphisms. -> The derived induction triangle and polynomial Yoneda algebra.
- **step-all-stable-degrees** [claim](#stmt-e-all-degrees) / [proof](#proof-stmt-e-all-degrees): Tensor resolutions to compute the external Yoneda algebra, then use the perfect composition pairing to obtain negative groups and transpose actions. -> The complete stable self-map profile and its negative multiplication maps.
- **step-socle-generator** [claim](#stmt-e-socle) / [proof](#proof-stmt-e-socle): Embed the simple through the explicit dual socle element and use symmetry to show that every projective factorization into the syzygy is zero. -> A concrete nonzero generator of the indicated negative stable group.
- **step-twist-weights** [claim](#stmt-e-twists) / [proof](#proof-stmt-e-twists): Compare twisted induction triangles for the positive weight, compute the socle weight directly, and use the perfect pairing for the remaining negative weights. -> Scalar twist actions on entire supported homogeneous spaces.
- **step-bar-representative** [claim](#stmt-b-bar-model) / [proof](#proof-stmt-b-bar-model): Compare the bar resolution with bounded resolutions separately on each side, split the specified high cokernel, and take finite bimodule cosyzygies. -> A fixed finite side-projective representative of shifted derived induction.
- **step-evaluated-profile** [claim](#stmt-b-profile) / [proof](#proof-stmt-b-profile): Tensor the specified triangle models, compute the polynomial Koszul sequence and its dual, and use the full attaching map in the successive long exact sequences. -> The sparse evaluated stable Hom profile.
- **step-ordinary-factorization** [claim](#stmt-b-ordinary-factorization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-b-ordinary-factorization): Replace perfect terms by functorial finite coinduced coresolutions and use the specified Ext orthogonalities to represent both derived arrows by ordinary chain maps. -> An ordinary factorization through the additive class of the dual-regular outer tensor bimodule.
- **step-trace-obstruction** [claim](#stmt-b-trace-obstruction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-b-trace-obstruction): Express ordinary composites as traces of multiplication operators; the radical part and even corner dimensions force vanishing inconsistent with the character map. -> Nonfactorization of the character map through a perfect complex.
- **step-stable-adjunction** [claim](#stmt-b-stable-adjunction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-b-stable-adjunction): Show ordinary adjunction preserves projective factorizations in both directions using a side-split projective-injective embedding of the source. -> A natural stable tensor–Hom adjunction.
- **step-evaluated-lift** [claim](#stmt-b-evaluated-lift) / [proof](#proof-stmt-b-evaluated-lift): Transpose composition using symmetric duality and rule out its vanishing by restricting a hypothetical perfect factorization to the character trace obstruction. -> A stable bimodule lift with nonzero evaluation for every nonzero twist parameter.
- **step-bimodule-and-lifts** [claim](#stmt-b-bimodule) / [proof](#proof-stmt-b-evaluated-lift-2): Combine the fixed finite representative with its evaluated profile, shift the nonzero lifts, and choose ordinary representatives over the original field. -> The fixed side-projective bimodule and genuine twist maps needed for the fiber.
- **step-comparison-ranges** [claim](#stmt-t-comparison-maps) / [proof](#proof-stmt-t-comparison-maps): Normalize the evaluated twist maps, form a side-split ordinary kernel, and project the comparison to scalar matrices; check boundary degrees separately. -> The required surjectivity, injectivity, and isomorphism ranges for the comparison maps.
- **step-triangular-cone** [claim](#stmt-t-finite-module) / [proof](#proof-stmt-t-finite-module): Lift the normalized fiber class to complete resolutions, apply the triangular column functors, and take its cone; use the upper component to detect nonprojectivity. -> A totally acyclic triangular complex with finite nonprojective cokernel.
- **step-cone-vanishing** [claim](#stmt-t-vanishing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-t-vanishing): Decompose the full cone endomorphism complex into diagonal and cross blocks, identify its connecting map with the comparison, and check both vanishing endpoints. -> The complete positive and negative self-Hom vanishing profile required by transfer.
- **step-main-counterexample** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-t-neighboring-degrees): Apply the general trivial-extension transfer to the finite triangular algebra and its nonprojective complete-resolution cokernel. -> The symmetric algebra and nonprojective self-orthogonal module of the main theorem.
- **step-endomorphism-dimensions** [claim](#stmt-prop-endomorphism) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-endomorphism): Apply the generator-cogenerator dominant-dimension criterion, use full faithfulness to exclude self-injectivity, and split a hypothetical finite injective resolution backwards. -> Infinite dominant and left self-injective dimensions of the opposite endomorphism algebra.
- **step-homological-consequences** [claim](#stmt-cor-homological) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-endomorphism-2): Preserve the symmetric pair and nonsplit extension class by faithful field extension, then select an omitted injective, its simple socle, and the projective-injective summands. -> The associated algebra and modules witnessing the homological conjecture failures.
- **step-finitistic-dimensions** [claim](#stmt-cor-finitistic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-endomorphism-3): Take successive cokernels of the projective-injective resolution and prove exact projective dimensions by dimension shifting and nonsplitting. -> Finite modules of every positive finite projective dimension and infinite left finitistic dimensions.
- **step-tilting-complements** [claim](#stmt-cor-tilting-complements) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-tilting-complements): Apply the cited cotilting-complement result to generalized Nakayama failure and dualize over the extension field. -> A projective almost complete tilting module with infinitely many pairwise nonisomorphic indecomposable complements.
- **step-idempotent-stratifications** [claim](#stmt-cor-stratifying) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-stratifying): Select a nonprojective indecomposable summand in a symmetric block and apply the cited reduced mirror-reflective constructions. -> Auxiliary indecomposable symmetric algebras with arbitrarily long idempotent stratifications and successive recollements.

In particular, two such models of the same derived object are homotopy equivalent.*\n\n\n*Suppose a derived triangle is given by $$L\\longrightarrow V\\longrightarrow R\\xrightarrow{\\partial}L[1].$$ For projective models $P_L,P_R$ and a chain map $b:P_R\\to P_L[1]$ representing $\\partial$, a model of $V$ is the two-cell complex $$\\label{h:two-cell}\n P_{V,j}=P_{L,j}\\oplus P_{R,j},\\qquad\n d_j^{P_V}=\\begin{pmatrix}d_j^{P_L}&b_j\\\\0&d_j^{P_R}\\end{pmatrix}.$$ Its subcomplex and quotient represent $L$ and $R$, and its connecting map is the specified $\\partial$.",

```json
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-h-stable-image",
      "depends_on": [

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-h-stable-image",
          "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be symmetric and let $P$ be bounded below with projective terms and bounded homology. For $n$ sufficiently large the formula $$\\label{h:stable-image-formula}\n \\langle P\\rangle=(c_nP)[n]$$ defines a stable module, independent of $n$ up to natural stable isomorphism. It is functorial on derived maps represented by such models, commutes with shifts, sends a module to its stable class, and sends an Ext class to its corresponding stable map.*\n\n*A degreewise split short exact sequence $0\\to P_L\\to P_V\\to P_R\\to0$ of these complexes gives a stable connecting map $\\langle P_R\\rangle\\to\\langle P_L\\rangle[1]$ and the associated long exact stable Hom sequences. With respect to a graded splitting, this connecting map is the stable image of the cross differential $b:P_R\\to P_L[1]$ in <a href=\"#h:two-cell\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"h:two-cell\">[h:two-cell]</a>.",
          "source_id": "PM-199.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-stable-images",
      "inputs": [
        "Bounded-below projective models with bounded homology"
      ],
      "method": "Pass to high cokernels above homology support and track the cross differential through the induced short exact sequences.",
      "output": "Functorial stable images with the actual connecting maps.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-h-stable-image"
    },

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-h-perfect-factor",

          "anchor": "stmt-h-perfect-factor",
          "exact_quote": "Over a symmetric algebra, a derived map between bounded derived objects whose stable image is zero factors in the derived category through a perfect complex.",

      "id": "step-perfect-factorization",

      "method": "Remove high components by a homotopy and factor the remaining chain map through a bounded projective truncation.",
      "output": "A perfect derived factorization.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-h-perfect-factor"
    },

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-h-evaluation",
      "depends_on": [

      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-h-evaluation",
          "exact_quote": "Every stable shift of a finite side-projective bimodule has a finite side-projective representative. Evaluation at a finite $E$-module $X$ sends stable maps between these bimodules to stable $E$-module maps and has natural identifications $$\\label{h:evaluation-shifts}\n (L[a])X\\simeq(LX)[a],\\qquad L(X[a])\\simeq(LX)[a].$$ It preserves the stable connecting maps of short exact sequences of side-projective bimodules, and these identifications are natural in both $L$ and $X$.*\n\n\n*If $P$ is a bounded-below complex of projective bimodules with bounded homology and all sufficiently high $c_nP$ are side-projective, then $P\\otimes_EX$ has bounded homology and $$\\label{h:evaluation-image}\n \\langle P\\rangle X\\simeq\\langle P\\otimes_EX\\rangle,$$ naturally on maps and on connecting maps from degreewise split sequences of such complexes. The hypothesis on the high cokernels holds whenever $P$, considered separately on either side, is homotopy equivalent to a bounded complex of projectives on that side.",
          "source_id": "PM-199.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-finite-evaluation",
      "inputs": [
        "Finite side-projective bimodules",
        "High side-projective cokernels"
      ],
      "method": "Use finite projective-injective embeddings and separate side splittings to construct shifts and preserve exactness under evaluation.",
      "output": "Finite representatives and evaluation compatible with shifts and attaching maps.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-h-evaluation"
    },

          "anchor": "stmt-a-dual-dimensions",
          "exact_quote": "The dual regular module $DC$ has projective dimension at most two on both sides. More precisely, $$D(eC)\\cong Ce,\\qquad D(Ce)\\cong eC,$$ and there are exact sequences $$\\begin{aligned}\n0\\longrightarrow Cf\\xrightarrow{\\rho_t}Ce\n \\xrightarrow{\\rho_{\\ell_{-2}}}Ce\n \\xrightarrow{e\\mapsto j^*}D(fC)\\longrightarrow0,\n\\label{a:left-dual-resolution}\\\\\n0\\longrightarrow fC\\xrightarrow{\\lambda_u}eC\n \\xrightarrow{\\lambda_{\\ell_0}}eC\n \\xrightarrow{e\\mapsto v^*}D(Cf)\\longrightarrow0.\n\\label{a:right-dual-resolution}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Consequently $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{pd}}_B({}_B E)\\le4,\\qquad\n\\mathop{\\mathrm{pd}}_{B^{\\mathrm{op}}}(E_B)\\le4,\\qquad\n\\mathop{\\mathrm{id}}_B({}_B B)\\le4.\n\\label{a:dimension-bounds}$$",
```

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-starting-algebra**: The displayed multiplication defines an associative unital algebra of dimension ten.
- **invariant-radical**: The radical is the span of the eight basis vectors other than $e,f$, and its fifth power vanishes.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-finite-ext-certificate**: Treat verification of the starting multiplication table as verification of the symmetric counterexample. -> Associativity and radical nilpotence do not establish the complete self-Hom vanishing required for transfer.
- **anti-parameter-specialization**: Replace the transcendental parameters by elements of a finite field and carry over the counterexample. -> Such a replacement does not preserve the stated algebraic independence hypothesis.
- **anti-side-projective-confusion**: Treat a bimodule that is projective separately on each side as projective over the enveloping algebra. -> Separate projectivity does not supply a bimodule projective factorization.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The executable scope is the finite multiplication-table construction in the starting-algebra lemma. Exhaustive exact checks establish its associativity, unit, radical ideal, nilpotence, and quotient structure. They do not certify the subsequent infinite-complex arguments or the main counterexample.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON with case equal to unit, associativity, or radical.
# PRECONDITIONS: The displayed basis and multiplication table; coefficients embed in the stated characteristic-two function field.
# OUTPUTS: JSON reporting the requested exhaustive algebra checks and basis dimension.
# TIME: Exhaustive finite basis enumeration and four radical-power multiplications; polynomial arithmetic is exact.
# SPACE: The multiplication table, finite spanning sets, and intermediate polynomial coefficients.
# ARITHMETIC: Binary polynomial encoding gives exact arithmetic in F_2[q], embedded in k.
# FAILURE: Invalid input raises an exception; failed mathematical checks are reported as false.
import json
import sys

names = 'e f x y z u v t j n'.split()
n = len(names)
index = {a:i for i,a in enumerate(names)}
zero = (0,) * n
basis = [tuple(int(i == j) for i in range(n)) for j in range(n)]

def pmul(a,b):
    r = 0
    while b:
        if b & 1:
            r ^= a
        a <<= 1
        b >>= 1
    return r

def add(a,b):
    return tuple(x ^ y for x,y in zip(a,b))

table = [[zero for _ in names] for _ in names]
def put(a,b,s):
    v = [0]*n
    for term in s.split(','):
        c,t = term.split(':')
        v[index[t]] ^= {'1':1,'q':2,'q2':4,'1+q':3}[c]
    table[index[a]][index[b]] = tuple(v)

for a in 'e x y z u v'.split():
    put('e',a,'1:'+a)
for a in 'f t j n'.split():
    put('f',a,'1:'+a)
for a in 'e x y z t j'.split():
    put(a,'e','1:'+a)
for a in 'f u v n'.split():
    put(a,'f','1:'+a)
put('x','y', 'q:z')
put('x','u','1:v')
put('y','x','1:z')
put('y','u','1:v')
put('u','t','1:y,q:x')
put('u','j','1:z')
put('u','n','1:v')
put('v','t','q:z')
put('t','x','1:j')
put('t','y','q2:j')
put('t','u','1+q:n')
put('n','t','q:j')

def mul(a,b):
    out = [0]*n
    for i,ai in enumerate(a):
        if ai:
            for j,bj in enumerate(b):
                if bj:
                    c = pmul(ai,bj)
                    for h,v in enumerate(table[i][j]):
                        if v:
                            out[h] ^= pmul(c,v)
    return tuple(out)

def evaluate(case):
    result = {'dimension':n}
    if case == 'unit':
        unit = add(basis[0], basis[1])
        result['unital'] = all(mul(unit,a) == a == mul(a,unit) for a in basis)
    elif case == 'associativity':
        result['associative'] = all(mul(mul(a,b),c) == mul(a,mul(b,c)) for a in basis for b in basis for c in basis)
    elif case == 'radical':
        rad = basis[2:]
        result['radical_ideal'] = all(mul(a,b)[:2] == (0,0) and mul(b,a)[:2] == (0,0) for a in rad for b in basis)
        powers = set(rad)
        for _ in range(4):
            powers = {mul(a,b) for a in powers for b in rad} - {zero}
        result['radical_fifth_zero'] = not powers
        result['semisimple_quotient'] = all(table[i][j][:2] == (tuple(int(h == i) for h in range(2)) if i == j else (0,0)) for i in range(2) for j in range(2))
    else:
        raise ValueError('unknown case')
    return result

request = json.load(sys.stdin)
print(json.dumps(evaluate(request['case']),sort_keys=True))
```

    if b & 1:\n            r ^= a\n        a <<= 1\n        b >>= 1\n    return r\n\ndef add(a,b):\n    return tuple(x ^ y for x,y in zip(a,b))\n\ntable = [[zero for _ in names] for _ in names]\ndef put(a,b,s):\n    v = [0]*n\n    for term in s.split(','):\n        c,t = term.split(':')\n        v[index[t]] ^= {'1':1,'q':2,'q2':4,'1+q':3}[c]\n    table[index[a]][index[b]] = tuple(v)\n\nfor a in 'e x y z u v'.split():\n    put('e',a,'1:'+a)\nfor a in 'f t j n'.split():\n    put('f',a,'1:'+a)\nfor a in 'e x y z t j'.split():\n    put(a,'e','1:'+a)\nfor a in 'f u v n'.split():\n    put(a,'f','1:'+a)\nput('x','y', 'q:z')\nput('x','u','1:v')\nput('y','x','1:z')\nput('y','u','1:v')\nput('u','t','1:y,q:x')\nput('u','j','1:z')\nput('u','n','1:v')\nput('v','t','q:z')\nput('t','x','1:j')\nput('t','y','q2:j')\nput('t','u','1+q:n')\nput('n','t','q:j')\n\ndef mul(a,b):\n    out = [0]*n\n    for i,ai in enumerate(a):\n        if ai:\n            for j,bj in enumerate(b):\n                if bj:\n                    c = pmul(ai,bj)\n                    for h,v in enumerate(table[i][j]):\n                        if v:\n                            out[h] ^= pmul(c,v)\n    return tuple(out)\n\ndef evaluate(case):\n    result = {'dimension':n}\n    if case == 'unit':\n        unit = add(basis[0], basis[1])\n        result['unital'] = all(mul(unit,a) == a == mul(a,unit) for a in basis)\n    elif case == 'associativity':\n        result['associative'] = all(mul(mul(a,b),c) == mul(a,mul(b,c)) for a in basis for b in basis for c in basis)\n    elif case == 'radical':\n        rad = basis[2:]\n        result['radical_ideal'] = all(mul(a,b)[:2] == (0,0) and mul(b,a)[:2] == (0,0) for a in rad for b in basis)\n        powers = set(rad)\n        for _ in range(4):\n            powers = {mul(a,b) for a in powers for b in rad} - {zero}\n        result['radical_fifth_zero'] = not powers\n        result['semisimple_quotient'] = all(table[i][j][:2] == (tuple(int(h == i) for h in range(2)) if i == j else (0,0)) for i in range(2) for j in range(2))\n    else:\n        raise ValueError('unknown case')\n    return result\n\nrequest = json.load(sys.stdin)\nprint(json.dumps(evaluate(request['case']),sort_keys=True))\n",
```json
    "test_vectors": [
      {

        "input": "{\"case\":\"radical\"}"
      }
    ]
```

| other (Representation theory and homological algebra) | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The paper constructs a finite side-projective bimodule from a bar-complex cokernel and successive finite cosyzygies. Its evaluated stable Hom profile and genuine bimodule lifts supply the fiber used in the triangular algebra construction. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-transcendental-base-field** (hypothesis): The counterexample is constructed over the stated characteristic-two function field with algebraically independent parameters. Persistence under field extension does not justify specializing those parameters. Blocked use: ['Claiming that parameter specialization produces the same counterexample over a finite field or its algebraic closure.']
- **limit-characteristic-and-indexing** (formal_scope): The displayed differentials and comparison maps use homological indexing and characteristic two, which removes signs. Blocked use: ['Reusing the displayed cone and tensor differentials in another characteristic without restoring and verifying signs.']
- **limit-full-hom-products** (formal_scope): Complete-resolution morphisms are computed using the full product Hom complex; finite-dimensional resolution terms do not imply finite-dimensional product terms. Blocked use: ['Replacing the full Hom complex by a direct sum or a finite window and asserting unchanged cohomology.']
- **limit-complete-comparison-preconditions** (hypothesis): Complete-resolution comparison requires total acyclicity of the source and an exact projective target complex. Blocked use: ['Applying the comparison to an arbitrary acyclic source without proving exactness of its Hom complex into the regular module.']
- **limit-bounded-below-derived-models** (formal_scope): The derived-map computation uses bounded-below projective models. The proof explicitly distinguishes these models from complete resolutions. Blocked use: ['Treating an arbitrary complete resolution as a projective model computing all derived morphisms.']
- **limit-stable-image-truncation** (hypothesis): Stable images require a symmetric algebra, a bounded-below projective model with bounded homology, and a cokernel sufficiently far above the homology support. Blocked use: ['Choosing an arbitrary truncation degree and assuming it defines the stable image.']
- **limit-side-projectivity-and-evaluation** (hypothesis): Evaluation and finite shift representatives use projectivity separately on both sides. This condition does not imply projectivity over the enveloping algebra. Blocked use: ['Using separate side projectivity as a bimodule projective factorization.', 'Applying the evaluation compatibilities without checking the required side splittings and high cokernels.']
- **limit-transfer-negative-tail** (hypothesis): Transfer requires a nonprojective cokernel of a totally acyclic complex and both the positive and the specified negative complete self-Hom vanishing ranges. Blocked use: ['Inferring self-orthogonality over the trivial extension from positive self-extension vanishing alone.', 'Dropping the negative endpoint needed to compute the first positive extension into the dual-induced summand.']
- **limit-tensor-exactness-is-resolution-specific** (formal_scope): The transfer proves tensor exactness for the chosen complete resolution; it does not establish unrestricted flatness of the trivial extension over the starting algebra. Blocked use: ['Replacing the resolution-specific argument by an unproved assertion that induction preserves every exact sequence.']
- **limit-parameter-shifting-resolution** (hypothesis): Exactness of the simple resolution depends on nonvanishing parameter-dependent coefficients and on the specified orientations of multiplication maps. Blocked use: ['Specializing the coefficient parameter and retaining the resolution without checking its images and kernels.', 'Interchanging left and right multiplication in the displayed resolutions.']
- **limit-bar-cokernel-side-splittings** (hypothesis): The finite bar representative depends on separate projective-dimension bounds and separate side splittings. The resulting side projectivity is not a bimodule splitting assertion. Blocked use: ['Declaring an arbitrary bar cokernel side-projective without the bounded-resolution comparison.', 'Replacing the separate side splittings by an assumed bimodule splitting.']

> ## Scope
>
> The Auslander–Reiten conjecture predicts that a finitely generated module $M$ over an Artin algebra $A$ is projective if $\mathrm{Ext}^i_A(M,M\oplus A)=0$ for every $i>0$. The formalized counterexample is a finite-dimensional algebra over $k=\mathbb F_2(q,H_1,H_2)$ and a finite-dimensional nonprojective Gorenstein-projective module with this vanishing.
>
> It also establishes $A/\mathrm{rad}\,A\cong k^8$, $(\mathrm{rad}\,A)^4\ne0$, and preservation of the listed properties after every field extension. The Tachikawa companion is separate.
>
> Tachikawa's second conjecture predicts that a finite-dimensional module over a finite-dimensional self-injective algebra is projective if all its positive-degree self-Ext groups vanish. The formalized counterexample gives a finite-dimensional symmetric algebra over $\mathbb F_2(q,H_1,H_2)$ and a finite-dimensional nonprojective module $M$ with $\mathrm{Ext}^i(M,M)=0$ for every $i>0$. Symmetric algebras are self-injective, so this contradicts the conjecture.
>
> The later field-extension, endomorphism-algebra, and related homological consequences are not included.

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

namespace Tachikawa

noncomputable section

abbrev k := FractionRing (MvPolynomial (Fin 3) (ZMod 2))

def SymmetricOver (K A : Type) [Field K] [Ring A] [Algebra K A] : Prop :=
  ∃ e : A ≃ₗ[K] Module.Dual K A,
    (∀ a b c : A, e (a * b) c = e b (c * a)) ∧
    (∀ a b c : A, e (a * b) c = e a (b * c))

def Counterexample : Prop :=
  ∃ (A : Type) (rA : Ring A),
    letI := rA
    ∃ (aA : Algebra k A),
      letI := aA
      Module.Finite k A ∧ SymmetricOver k A ∧
      ∃ (M : Type) (gM : AddCommGroup M),
        letI := gM
        ∃ (mA : Module A M) (mk : Module k M),
          letI := mA
          letI := mk
          IsScalarTower k A M ∧ Module.Finite k M ∧
          ¬ Module.Projective A M ∧
          ∀ n : ℕ, 0 < n →
            Subsingleton (CategoryTheory.Abelian.Ext (ModuleCat.of A M) (ModuleCat.of A M) n)

theorem main_theorem : Counterexample := by
  sorry

end

end Tachikawa

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We disprove Tachikawa's second conjecture by constructing a finite-dimensional symmetric algebra over $`k=\mathbb F_2(q,H_1,H_2)`$ with a finite-dimensional nonprojective module whose self-extension groups vanish in every positive degree. The associated endomorphism algebra also gives counterexamples to the classical, generalized, and strong Nakayama conjectures, the Auslander–Gorenstein conjecture, and the Wakamatsu tilting conjecture. These conclusions persist after every extension of <i>k</i>.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Let $A$ be a finite-dimensional algebra over a field $k$. A finite-dimensional $A$-module $M$ is *self-orthogonal* if $\mathop{\mathrm{Ext}}_A^i(M,M)=0$ for every integer $i>0$. Tachikawa’s second conjecture asserts that every finite-dimensional self-orthogonal module over a finite-dimensional self-injective algebra is projective. This question belongs to the homological conjectures surrounding quasi-Frobenius algebras and dominant dimension; see Tachikawa’s 1973 monograph (Tachikawa 1973, 351:VI–VII) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Tachikawa1973](#ref-Tachikawa1973)] and the account of the conjecture in (Chen and Xi 2025, 7288) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[ChenXi2025TAMS](#ref-ChenXi2025TAMS)]. The Auslander–Reiten conjecture, originating in (Auslander and Reiten 1975) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[AuslanderReiten1975](#ref-AuslanderReiten1975)], asks for finite-dimensional algebras whether the condition $\mathop{\mathrm{Ext}}_A^i(M,M\oplus A)=0$ for every $i>0$ forces $M$ to be projective. For a self-injective algebra, the groups $\mathop{\mathrm{Ext}}_A^i(M,A)$ vanish automatically, so Tachikawa’s second conjecture is its self-injective case.

Write $D=\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_k(-,k)$. An algebra $A$ is *symmetric* if $A\simeq DA$ as $A$-bimodules; in particular, a symmetric algebra is self-injective. We prove the following result in this class.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *Let $$k=\mathbb F_2(q,H_1,H_2),$$ where $q,H_1,H_2$ are algebraically independent. There exist a finite-dimensional associative unital symmetric $k$-algebra $A$ and a finite-dimensional unital left $A$-module $M$ such that $M$ is nonprojective and $$\mathop{\mathrm{Ext}}_A^i(M,M)=0\qquad\text{for every integer }i>0.$$ In particular, Tachikawa’s second conjecture is false.*

</div>

Since $A$ is self-injective, the same module satisfies $\mathop{\mathrm{Ext}}_A^i(M,M\oplus A)=0$ for every $i>0$. Thus Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> also gives a symmetric counterexample to the Auslander–Reiten conjecture.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
###### Related results.

Schulz’s earlier example of a nonprojective module without positive self-extensions uses an algebra over a skew field, outside the finite-dimensional-over-a-field setting considered here; see (Schulz 1994) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Schulz1994](#ref-Schulz1994)] and the distinction recalled in (Diveris and Purin 2012, Introduction) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[DiverisPurin2012](#ref-DiverisPurin2012)]. His quantum-exterior example has nonzero first self-extension but no higher self-extensions (Schulz 1995, Example 7) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Schulz1995](#ref-Schulz1995)]; the parameter-shifting syzygies in that example are an antecedent for our starting algebra. For symmetric Artin algebras, Diveris and Purin show that a component containing a nonprojective module with eventually vanishing self-extensions has stable part of type $\mathbb Z A_\infty$ (Diveris and Purin 2014, Theorem A) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[DiverisPurin2014](#ref-DiverisPurin2014)]. Over an algebraically closed field, Erdmann classifies, up to Morita equivalence, the indecomposable weakly symmetric algebras with radical cube zero and nonzero radical square that admit such modules. The classification singles out quantum exterior and double Nakayama algebras with parameters that are not roots of unity (Erdmann 2017, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Erdmann2017](#ref-Erdmann2017)].

Chen, Fang and Xi characterize the symmetric case through stratifying ideals and give conditional mirror-reflective constructions from gendo-symmetric counterexamples to the Nakayama conjecture (Chen, Fang, and Xi 2024, Theorems 1.1 and 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[ChenFangXi2024](#ref-ChenFangXi2024)]. Chen and Xi relate self-orthogonal Nakayama-stable generators over self-injective algebras to additional categorical and Gorenstein–Morita conditions (Chen and Xi 2025, Theorem 1.2 and Corollary 1.4) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[ChenXi2025TAMS](#ref-ChenXi2025TAMS)]. Their further criterion assumes virtual Gorensteinness of the relevant endomorphism algebra (Chen and Xi 2026, Theorem 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[ChenXi2025VG](#ref-ChenXi2025VG)]. Zhang and Zhou prove the self-injective case for split algebras with radical cube zero (X. Zhang and Zhou 2026b, Corollary 4.2) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[ZhangZhou2026](#ref-ZhangZhou2026)] and subsequently treat Igusa–Todorov algebras (X. Zhang and Zhou 2026a, Theorem 3.2) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[ZhangZhouIgusaTodorov2026](#ref-ZhangZhouIgusaTodorov2026)]. Xia proves the Auslander–Reiten conjecture for quantum complete intersections (Xia 2026, Corollary 4.3) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Xia2026](#ref-Xia2026)]. In a different direction, Böhmler and Marczinzik construct a nonprojective module over a commutative local self-injective algebra whose first two self-extension groups vanish, while the third does not (Böhmler and Marczinzik 2026, Theorem 3.1 and the end of Section 3) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[BohmlerMarczinzik2026](#ref-BohmlerMarczinzik2026)]. Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> establishes vanishing in every positive degree.

Enomoto and Marczinzik prove that Tachikawa’s second conjecture for the two-fold trivial extension of an Artin algebra implies the Auslander–Reiten conjecture for that algebra (Enomoto and Marczinzik 2026, Theorem 3.5) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[Enomoto2026](#ref-Enomoto2026)]. This reduction has a self-injective target, not a symmetric target in general. Our construction obtains a symmetric algebra by establishing the additional complete-resolution vanishing needed for transfer to the ordinary trivial extension. Enomoto and Marczinzik also prove an equivalence among the universal versions of the Auslander–Reiten, generalized Nakayama, Nakayama, and both Tachikawa conjectures over a fixed commutative Artinian coefficient ring (Enomoto and Marczinzik 2026, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[Enomoto2026](#ref-Enomoto2026)].

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
###### The construction.

The companion article (OpenAI 2026b) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[OpenAIAR2026](#ref-OpenAIAR2026)] constructs an explicit eight-simple triangular counterexample. We use the same ten-dimensional starting algebra and related cone and triangular-module constructions, and reproduce the algebra, resolutions, and arguments needed here. The passage to a symmetric algebra requires vanishing in negative degrees of a complete self-extension complex as well as positive Ext vanishing.

<a id="intro:profile"></a>
Two steps organize the proof. First, Proposition <a href="#tr:transfer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="tr:transfer">11</a> gives a general transfer principle. For a finite-dimensional algebra $\Lambda$, a doubly infinite exact complex $P_Z$ of finite projective $\Lambda$-modules is *totally acyclic* if $\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_\Lambda(P_Z,\Lambda)$ is also exact. Suppose $P_Z$ is totally acyclic, $Z=\mathop{\mathrm{coker}}((P_Z)_1\to(P_Z)_0)$, and $$\label{intro:profile}
 H^a\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_\Lambda(P_Z,Z)=0
 \qquad(a>0\ \text{or}\ a\leq-2).$$ Then the trivial extension and induced module $$A=\Lambda\ltimes D\Lambda,
 \qquad M=A\otimes_\Lambda Z$$ give the required symmetric example whenever $Z$ is nonprojective. The negative tail computes the positive extensions into $D\Lambda\otimes_\Lambda Z$, with the precise index $-a-1$. This transfer principle applies to any finite-dimensional algebra and module satisfying its hypotheses.

Second, we construct the data required by this transfer. A ten-dimensional algebra $C$ has a one-dimensional simple module $s$. Put $$T=C\ltimes DC,\qquad B=C\otimes_k C,
 \qquad E=T\otimes_k T,\qquad X=s\otimes_k s.$$ We regard $s$ as a $T$-module through $T\to C$, and $X$ as an $E$-module. Write $s[r]$ for $s$ placed in homological degree $r$. Derived induction of $s$ from $C$ to $T$ gives an object $\mathcal J=T\otimes_C^{\mathbf L}s$ in a triangle $$s[2]\longrightarrow\mathcal J\longrightarrow s
 \xrightarrow{\tau}s[3].$$ The class $\tau$ generates $\mathop{\mathrm{Ext}}_T^*(s,s)=k[\tau]$. The tensor product $E$ is symmetric, and external products give the self-extension algebra $\mathop{\mathrm{Ext}}_E^*(X,X)=k[\tau_1,\tau_2]$, where $\tau_1=\tau\otimes1$ and $\tau_2=1\otimes\tau$ both have degree three. A stable map is a module map modulo maps factoring through projectives; over a symmetric algebra the shift $[1]$ is inverse syzygy. Symmetric duality determines the negative stable self-maps of $X$ from this polynomial algebra.

Tensoring two copies of this triangle describes $E\otimes_B^{\mathbf L}X\simeq\mathcal J\otimes\mathcal J$. Its attaching maps act by $\tau_1$ and $\tau_2$. On nonnegative stable self-maps these give the two-variable Koszul sequence, and on negative self-maps they give its dual. This Koszul calculation, together with the full attaching maps, leaves only two scalar groups. The bar complex for $E\otimes_B^{\mathbf L}E$ provides a finite $E$-bimodule $Y$, projective separately on the left and right, whose evaluation represents $(\mathcal J\otimes\mathcal J)[-1]$ in the stable category. Its stable Hom groups are $$W^a:=\underline{\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}}_E(X,(Y\otimes_E X)[a])\simeq
 \begin{cases}k,&a=-3,0,\\0,&a\notin\{-3,0\}.\end{cases}$$ For $H\in k^\times$, let $h_H$ fix $C$ and multiply $DC$ by $H$, and let $U_H$ be $E$ with right action twisted by $h_H\otimes h_H$. There is a natural identification $U_H\otimes_E X=X$. To combine two twists, we need the nonzero line $W^0$ to be reached by evaluating genuine bimodule maps $U_H\to Y$; a module map $X\to Y\otimes_E X$ alone does not supply a bimodule fiber. A trace obstruction for a character map $B\to DB$, combined with symmetric duality, produces precisely these maps $g_H$, with nonzero evaluations in degree zero. The sparse Hom profile and these bimodule lifts are the two conclusions of Proposition <a href="#b:bimodule" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="b:bimodule">19</a>.

After normalizing the maps for $H_1,H_2$, their fiber has a finite bimodule representative $F$, obtained as an ordinary kernel after adjoining a projective summand. It comes with a stable map $v:X\to F\otimes_E X$. The two twists act by distinct scalars on each nonzero positive or negative homogeneous self-map space of $X$. These actions make the self-extension comparison invertible in the required degree ranges. Forming a cone over $$\Lambda=\begin{pmatrix}E&0\\ F&E\end{pmatrix}$$ produces the totally acyclic resolution of $Z$ with <a href="#intro:profile" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="intro:profile">[intro:profile]</a>; the boundary degrees $1$ and $-2$ are checked separately. Figure <a href="#fig:proof-architecture" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:proof-architecture">1</a> locates the bimodule construction and the final transfer in this argument. All bimodule representatives, kernels, and cokernels used to define the final algebra and module are finite-dimensional over the stated field. Infinite complexes serve to prove their properties.

<figure id="fig:proof-architecture">

<figcaption>Constructions and implications in the proof. The complete self-Hom vanishing for $(Λ,Z)$ supplies the hypotheses for transfer to $(A,M)$. Arrows indicate proof stages, not algebra maps.</figcaption>
</figure>

<a id="pm-figsrc-fig-proof-architecture"></a>

```latex
 {$Y$ and $g_H:U_H\to Y$\\$W^a=0$ for $a\notin\{-3,0\}$};
\node[box] (fv) at (6.6,1)
 {$F$ and $v:X\to F\otimes_E X$};
\node[box] (lz) at (0,1)
 {$(\Lambda,Z)$ and $P_Z$\\$H^a\Hom_\Lambda(P_Z,Z)=0$\\for $a>0$ or $a\leq-2$};
\node[box] (am) at (0,-1.6)
 {$A=\Lambda\ltimes D\Lambda$\\$M=A\otimes_\Lambda Z$\\symmetric counterexample};
\draw[->] (ex)--node[above=9mm,lab]{derived induction\\and trace obstruction}(y);
\draw[->] (y)--node[right,lab]{two twists\\and finite fiber}(fv);
\draw[->] (fv)--node[above=9mm,lab]{triangular cone}(lz);
\draw[->] (lz)--node[right,lab]{trivial extension\\and induction}(am);
\end{tikzpicture}
```

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
###### Homological consequences.

The pair $(A,M)$ gives counterexamples to several conjectures formulated through injective resolutions and tilting modules. Corollary <a href="#cor:homological" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:homological">2</a> realizes five such failures using one endomorphism algebra and two associated modules. We state the relevant conjectures in their finite-dimensional left-module forms before giving the corollary. For a finite-dimensional algebra $\Gamma$, write $$0\longrightarrow {}_\Gamma\Gamma\longrightarrow I^0
 \longrightarrow I^1\longrightarrow\cdots$$ for its minimal injective resolution. Its *dominant dimension* is the supremum of the integers $d\geq0$ for which $I^j$ is projective whenever $0\leq j<d$, and is infinite when every $I^j$ is projective. The classical *Nakayama conjecture* asserts that infinite dominant dimension forces self-injectivity. The *generalized Nakayama conjecture* asserts that every indecomposable injective occurs as a summand of some $I^j$; equivalently, for every simple module $S$, some $\mathop{\mathrm{Ext}}_\Gamma^i(S,\Gamma)$ with $i\geq0$ is nonzero. The *strong Nakayama conjecture* asserts that a finite module $V$ with $\mathop{\mathrm{Ext}}_\Gamma^i(V,\Gamma)=0$ for all $i\geq0$ is zero. The *Auslander–Gorenstein conjecture* asserts that the bounds $\mathop{\mathrm{pd}}_\Gamma I^j\leq j$ for every $j\geq0$ force finite self-injective dimension on both sides. These formulations are recalled in (Y. Zhang 2018, Introduction) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[Zhang2018HomologicalConjectures](#ref-Zhang2018HomologicalConjectures)].

Write $\mathop{\mathrm{add}}W$ for the direct summands of finite direct sums of a module $W$. A finite left $\Gamma$-module $W$ is *tilting* if it has finite projective dimension, is self-orthogonal, and admits a finite exact coresolution $$0\longrightarrow\Gamma\longrightarrow W^0\longrightarrow\cdots
 \longrightarrow W^r\longrightarrow0,\qquad W^j\in\mathop{\mathrm{add}}W.$$ It is *Wakamatsu tilting* if it is self-orthogonal and admits an exact coresolution $$0\longrightarrow\Gamma\longrightarrow W^0\longrightarrow W^1
 \longrightarrow\cdots,\qquad W^j\in\mathop{\mathrm{add}}W,$$ which remains exact after applying $\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_\Gamma(-,W)$. This is the finite left-module definition in (Huang 2006, Definition 2.1) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[Huang2006](#ref-Huang2006)]. The *Wakamatsu tilting conjecture* asserts that a Wakamatsu tilting module of finite projective dimension is tilting; see (Divaani-Aazar, Mahin Fallah, and Tousi 2025) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[DivaaniAazarMahinFallahTousi2025](#ref-DivaaniAazarMahinFallahTousi2025)] for a recent discussion.

<a id="stmt-cor-homological"></a>
<div id="cor:homological" class="corollary">

**Corollary 2** (Homological conjectures). *Let $A,M$ be as in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>. For every field extension $K/k$, put $A_K=K\otimes_k A$, $M_K=K\otimes_k M$, and $$\Gamma_K=\mathop{\mathrm{End}}_{A_K}(A_K\oplus M_K)^{\mathrm{op}}.$$ Then $\Gamma_K$ is a finite-dimensional $K$-algebra with the following properties, in the category of finite left $\Gamma_K$-modules.*

1.  *Every term of the minimal injective resolution of $\Gamma_K$ is projective, but $\mathop{\mathrm{id}}_{\Gamma_K}\Gamma_K=\infty$.*

2.  *There is a nonzero simple module $S$ such that $\mathop{\mathrm{Ext}}_{\Gamma_K}^i(S,\Gamma_K)=0$ for every $i\geq0$.*

3.  *There is a projective-injective Wakamatsu tilting module $W$ which is not tilting.*

*Consequently the classical, generalized, and strong Nakayama conjectures, the Auslander–Gorenstein conjecture, and the Wakamatsu tilting conjecture are false for finite-dimensional algebras over $K$.*

</div>
